Title: A Treatise on Atonement; in which the finite nature of sin is argued, its cause and consequences as such; the necessity and nature of atonement; and its glorious consequences, in the final reconciliation of all men to holiness and happiness
Creator: Hosea Ballou, of Barnard; ordained pastor of the united societies of Barnard, Woodstock, Hartland, Bethel and Bridgewater
Publisher: Randolph (Vt.): printed by Sereno Wright
Publication Date: 1805
Rights: Public Domain (published 1805, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item's own rights statement reads 'NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT')
Source: Internet Archive item atreatiseonaton06ballgoog (https://archive.org/details/atreatiseonaton06ballgoog), the item's own atreatiseonaton06ballgoog_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: University of Michigan; host metadata date: 1805. Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; the host's front matter up to and including the line beginning 'at |http: //books' removed (47 lines); nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. University of Michigan copy scanned by Google; good OCR, long s as f. Google's usage notice heads the djvu text and is removed through its last line.
Content note: Ballou's case for universal reconciliation: sin as finite, atonement as God's reconciling of man rather than a satisfaction to God, and Christ as a dependent mediator, not 'the self-existent Jehovah'; the census names it.
